Master list — every public GitHub Pages site

One block per site, in a fixed order: the live link, a description quoted from the repository's own files, the date created, the date of the last default-branch commit, the recorded Pages build status, and the exact official endpoints to re-check that row by hand. Nothing is invented here: every value is re-emitted from the committed snapshot by tools/build_master_list.py, and the page loads no data and runs no code.

What this list covers

  • 156 public repositories were read through the official API for buffedlizard55-lab at the snapshot below.
  • 156 of them have GitHub Pages enabled and are the census this list must account for.
  • 155 are published below, one block each.
  • 1 repository is permanently excluded by owner instruction: it has no row, no link, no description and no category on this page, and it is never counted as a published site.
  • The accounting balances: 155 listed + 1 not published = 156 Pages sites = 156 public repositories. This generator refuses to write the page when that sum stops balancing or when a repository is public but neither published nor excluded, so a stale or partial snapshot cannot be presented as a complete list.
  • kanlerxz87-cyber reports 0 public repositories, therefore zero public Pages sites; that result comes from GET /users/kanlerxz87-cyber.
  • Private repositories are out of scope: the public API cannot enumerate them, so no claim is made about them.

How to check any row by hand

Every block on this page links the endpoints it was built from. Open them in order and the row can be reproduced without trusting this page or the generator.

  1. Queried GET https://api.github.com/users/{login}/repos for both specified accounts (buffedlizard55-lab returned 156 public repositories; kanlerxz87-cyber returned 0).
  2. Queried GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/{repo} for every repository to extract created_at, pushed_at, updated_at, default_branch, size and metadata.
  3. Queried GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/{repo}/pages for every repository to confirm Pages status, custom-domain configuration and the published branch/path.
  4. Queried paginated GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/{repo}/commits?sha={default_branch} to obtain exact commit totals plus first-commit and latest-commit timestamps and SHAs.
  5. Queried GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/{repo}/contents/{published path}/index.html; an existing index.html is classified as an HTML-entry-point site ('app' in the data schema), while an absent one is classified as a README/documentation stub. This does not assert that a site is interactive.
  6. Titles, categories, descriptions and flags are curated in tools/overlay.json from each repository's own files. This metadata generator does not fetch README prose or infer descriptions; verifiedBasis records the source evidence and verifiedAtSha records the commit the prose was checked against.
  7. New public Pages repositories without a curated overlay entry are recorded in counts.unlisted and are not described or added to sites[]. Previously verified unreachable entries are carried from tools/overlay.json and should be rechecked with tools/verify_live.py before publication.
  8. 156 public repositories carry a GitHub Pages site; this directory publishes 155 of them, and every other repository is named with the reason it is withheld (ProjX: permanently excluded by owner request). 1 repo(s) are permanently excluded by owner request and must never be added back (see AGENTS.md); any other unlisted repository is one that has no curated, sourced entry yet and is therefore never described.
  9. Observed anomalies are retained in the irregularities register with source endpoints for manual review; the register does not imply that every possible defect in every upstream repository has been found.
  10. The generated 'proseStale' field is a mechanical SHA comparison: it means the repository head differs from the commit at which the description was last checked, not that the text has been proved false. A matching SHA is provenance, not proof that every sentence is correct.
  11. tools/audit_kind.py independently re-derives the HTML-entry-point vs. stub classification from the Pages API source path and contents endpoint, then compares it with tools/overlay.json.

Limits a reviewer should keep in mind

  • Created date is GitHub's repository created_at timestamp. The first commit is a separate observation and can differ by more than one second; both timestamps are shown rather than treated as interchangeable.
  • The directory's Last Commit date is the newest committer timestamp on the default branch. pushed_at tracks the latest push to any branch, and GitHub updated_at can reflect other repository activity. The public API does not expose when people last visited or used a Pages site; no last-use date is claimed.
  • Repository size is GitHub's own size field in KB; it is recomputed asynchronously and can lag or lead the commit history.
  • Descriptions are curated from repository-owned README/source files, not generated from repo names. A description whose recorded SHA differs from the snapshot head is marked stale for re-reading; the SHA difference alone does not prove the prose is inaccurate.
  • This audit queries public repositories only. Private repositories are not enumerated, so the directory makes no claim about private GitHub Pages sites.
  • The audit sandbox can reach github.com but not *.github.io, so live page bodies are not re-fetched over HTTP here. 'Built' status from the Pages API plus an HTML entry-point check are API-level signals, not a guarantee that the public page responds or works; every entry links to its live site for review.
  • kanlerxz87-cyber has 0 public repositories and therefore zero public GitHub Pages sites.

Verification-first election intelligence project using free, public, official data only: it backtests polls and prediction markets against verified official outcomes, flags every irregularity it finds, and runs a paper-trading forecasting contest, reverse-engineered from TradingView's The Leap, on Kalshi's open political and election markets. State counted from the repository's own files at commit dae1427: data/sources/master.json holds 267 entries (258 verified, 3 verified-claim, 2 verified-via-search, 4 needs-review), each carrying a manual-review link, the observed verification text, a date, a status and a category — and the README's banner and source table both agree with that file at 267, which closes the 209-versus-189-versus-229 divergence this directory recorded as IRR-67. The README's two other count complaints are closed too, and one of them closes a defect in this directory's own earlier entry: the README describes data/master_sources.json as 20 live-only entries and data/flagged_sources.json as 7 excluded sources, and the files hold exactly 20 and 7 — the previous version of this entry published "6 and 4", which was len() of each file's top-level dict (6 keys and 4 keys) rather than a count of its sources array, corrected here (IRR-95). The banner's '84 irregularities' is the highest id in data/irregularities.json, which holds 74 items. The 2024 market backtest covers 39 settled markets: at T-1 the 37 priced markets gave a mean Brier score of 0.077 and mean log-loss 0.271 with the favourite winning 34 of 37 (91.9 percent), while at T-7 the 25 markets gave Brier 0.104 with the favourite right 23 of 25. The captured open universe for the live 2026 layer is data/kalshi/forward/universe-open.json, which at this head holds 24,141 markets from 4,226 of 4,226 eligible series, complete true, captured 2026-09-23T00:33:32.866Z — while the README still prints 24,150 in its file table, 24,102 in its canonical-list section and 24,139 in its All Markets paragraph, so the file is the number of record (IRR-96). Daily collection runs on cron 12:30 UTC plus a push trigger. The site gained an All Markets page over the full captured universe, npm test reports 163 tests, and the live 2026 contest (R16, season S1-2026, opened 2026-09-19) fields 12 entrants with 41 tests of its own, 3 of the 12 having met the 3-day minimum over 5 captured trading days. GitHub Pages reported errored for two consecutive builds before the previous head and reports built at this one (IRR-83).

Quoted preview: the first 300 characters, cut mid-sentence because no sentence boundary occurs that early. The full sourced description is 3,601 characters — expand to read every word published in the directory and check it by hand.

Source-linked GitHub Pages guide to shoulder and shoulder-blade ('wing') pain, written around one stated context — a male adult aged about 36-40, left-side sleeping, a possible workout injury, prolonged computer use and limited room space — and explicitly educational information rather than a diagnosis or a personalised rehabilitation plan, with emergency and urgent warning signs given priority over exercises, furniture or symptom logging. Ten pages share one static template and compact navigation: start, medical advice, treatment, exercises, desk and room, furniture and prices, care costs, an optional log, sources and audit, and next steps; all research content works without JavaScript and no sign-in or symptom entry is required. The current revision is a deliberate narrowing rather than an expansion. It reviewed the pre-existing site at commit e1c63c4 and, in its own words, 'replaces blanket verification/completeness claims with a narrower, auditable source register, simplifies the UI and corrects unsafe or unverified assumptions', keeping the previous site in Git history. Twenty official pages were opened directly on 2026-09-20 — MedlinePlus, NHS, AAOS/ASES, CSP, OSHA, CMS, five furniture manufacturers or sellers, and Luna — and data/sources.json records each source's section, a short excerpt, its scope and its check date, with government and medical-society guidance kept separate from commercial product information. data/claims.json maps source-bearing content units to references and source-file lines: 90 review units, described as a review aid and 'not an automated truth certificate'. Exercises were reduced to four source-checked examples carrying exact AAOS HTML doses and supervision boundaries. Five furniture listings give exact variant and price boundaries with folding trade-offs, and no product is claimed to fit an unmeasured room or to treat shoulder pain; the one care price quoted is an official provider example ($135 self-pay per visit at Luna), not a nationwide average, and other unverified medical costs are explicitly left unpriced. REVIEW.md documents three passes and eight source irregularities found while reading, including a COMHOMA price where $54.49 was the selected White variant and $25.50 the discount, a VIVO desk that folds against the wall but is not quickly detached, a Lillipad whose specifications claim a 700 lb static test while its FAQ says 350 lb and neither replaces the 75 lb operational rating, and a Luna search excerpt showing $125 where both directly opened pages showed $135. The optional log is local-only, retains the `shoulderpain-log-v1` key so prior entries can still be read or imported if valid, and validates numbers, booleans, calendar dates and shape; invalid entries are counted and never rounded or silently converted, invalid persisted data blocks edits rather than being overwritten, raw JSON export is available for recovery, cross-tab changes block saves until reload, spreadsheet formula injection is mitigated on CSV export, and the bounded import size was raised to 20 MB so the 5,000-entry schema can import its own backups. Its former clinical 'improving/worsening' classifier and calendar-based advice were removed on the grounds that sparse logs should not reassure someone or postpone assessment, and the summary is now descriptive only. Validation reports 8 Node unit tests and 31 Chromium browser tests passing, automated axe WCAG 2 A/AA and 2.1 AA checks passing on all ten pages at 390px and 1440px, and no page-level horizontal overflow at 320, 390, 768 or 1440px. Sources retrieved 2026-09-20.

Quoted preview: the first 110 characters, cut at a sentence boundary. The full sourced description is 1,950 characters — expand to read every word published in the directory and check it by hand.

Evidence-first paper-trading lab for Kalshi event contracts, organised around an automated forward-test desk. 28 live return-seeking strategy personas plus 2 deliberately gated ones (Form4Flash, SpreadSmith) trade open Kalshi contracts on paper from a scheduled, read-only GitHub Actions collector — a cheap tick every five minutes aimed at in-game markets and a full cycle twice an hour at :07 and :37 — and every fill, exit, settlement, intent, mark and order book is committed back to the repository. Two backtests run on verified official Kalshi price data: the original hand-collected sample (3 settled markets, 130 verified bars, 5 personas), deliberately frozen, and an archive backtest replayed bar by bar over the candle archive the collector now stores, with per-trade equity curves and a walk-forward stability split. An execution-realism check re-reads every simulated fill against Kalshi's own published trade tape via GET /markets/trades and reports the difference in cents, with a committed live result of 156 fills compared at a median absolute desk-minus-tape gap of 1.00 cent, 74.36 percent within 1 cent of a real print, 69.23 percent inside the tape's real range and 65.38 percent covered by tape volume. A season health audit (scripts/audit_season.py) checks the schedule, ledger coverage, storage compaction, signal adapters and the tape summary, re-reads settled markets against the official API on the runner, and refuses to let verify_data.py pass if anything disagrees. Simulation only: nothing submits orders to Kalshi and no API key exists anywhere in the project. IRREGULARITY: the README's own archive-backtest headline (63 markets, 2,455 verified bars, 291 trades) does not match the committed generated files at the same head, which hold 74 markets, 2,736 verified bars and 337 trade rows across 266 distinct trade ids; both sets of figures are published here and the generated files are the number of record (IRR-97).

Quoted preview: the first 219 characters, cut at a sentence boundary. The full sourced description is 3,473 characters — expand to read every word published in the directory and check it by hand.

Biopharma decision engine tracking publicly traded companies against FDA drug decisions, verified stock-price reactions and clinical-pipeline success rates, rendered as a dependency-free static site with no build step. The project is now at v29 (2026-09-22) and the whole-table census has landed: the official Drugs@FDA Submissions.txt holds 193,810 rows, and the boundary the project defends holds over all of them — 0 rows are dated between the FD&C Act (1938-06-25) and 1938-12-31. That census also contradicted two of the project's own v28 working hypotheses and v29 publishes the contradictions verbatim rather than smoothing them: the before-1939 window holds 1 row rather than 0 (ApplNo 060904, ORIG 1, AP, dated 1900-01-01, a pre-statute placeholder published as FLAG-PRE-STATUTE-DATE with counted_as_fda_decision false), the undated window holds 8 rows (1 ORIG on 009658 plus 7 SUPPL) enumerated as FLAG-UNDATED-ROW, and the arithmetic 193,810 = 1 + 8 + 193,801 is stated in the artifact. The status census over the complete table is AP 192,604 / TA 1,205 / empty 1 — not the AP-only census v28 expected — with TA defined verbatim from FDA's own Drugs@FDA glossary as a tentative approval that does not allow marketing, so the project's wording was refined from 'approval actions only' to 'approval-family actions only (AP, TA)'; the type census is ORIG 27,862 / SUPPL 165,948 and the builder now aborts on any status outside {AP, TA, empty}. ApplNos 060904 and 009658 have no row in the official Applications table in either publication, appear in no committed window and in no project table, and render no application section on their official overview pages — no cause is inferred. Two corrections are recorded as corrections, not as footnotes: v28 wrote that NDA000159 was already counted in data/pre1965_fda_decisions.csv, which was false (that table holds NME-comparable rows only, earliest 1942, and NDA000159 is row PRE1965AUDIT-1939-02 of the pre-1965 originals audit table), and the same error class had already been caught for NDA000004 in v28. A runner defect that made Actions runs 32 and 33 fail was found and fixed: zip_extract re-downloaded the official ZIP on every run and overwrote the SHA-pinned window extracts, so every SHA-gated builder aborted and the workflow's if: always() commit step pushed the drifted payloads to the feature branch after the PR had merged; zip_extract now honours skip_existing and a run that produces only skipped-existing stubs leaves manifest.json byte-identical, and a failed page-0 fetch in the paged openFDA kinds no longer writes a 0-record payload with status 200 over the committed file. Autonomous live book-maker lab for prediction markets, focused on pricing theory rather than profit: it builds a public-data-only order-book model for Kalshi contracts (books, candles, schedules, settlements, market metadata, series and fees) and models the three things a bookmaker must decide — the…

Quoted preview: the first 300 characters, cut mid-sentence because no sentence boundary occurs that early. The full sourced description is 2,552 characters — expand to read every word published in the directory and check it by hand.

Autonomous live book-maker lab for prediction markets, focused on pricing theory rather than profit: it builds a public-data-only order-book model for Kalshi contracts (books, candles, schedules, settlements, market metadata, series and fees) and models the three things a bookmaker must decide — the fair price, the price to quote and the price at which to stop quoting. The model assumes an order-flow process whose initial estimate of the fair value is wrong, models the informed side as having observed the outcome while the uninformed side is noisy, states a pure market-maker objective of capturing the spread, and prices an American binary option by valuing the asset at exercise and converting to an event probability through the inverse CDF — the midpoint between the bid and the ask is a price, not an expectation. It publishes a scoring guide for spread captures (a continuous book with depth at every price scores a capture, a depleted or one-sided book scores a price change, a stale book scores no capture), a venue-analysis guide framed as mid-price precision and a mirror-fill cost, a 61-category research log with the explicit caveat that a category is a hypothesis class rather than a conclusion, and a stated limit that thin books inflate Kelly fractions and that public-data candles are not always printable. data/candles/ holds 84 committed candle files (53 KXBTC, 31 KXETHD) from 12 series, including every contract month under the KXBTCD and KXETHD series with official fetch provenance, and data/manifest.json's series-catalog entry is a 17,505,797-byte full series catalog with volume, sha256 af04b45aabd739cd0d3f78d782b327cdb72e7c23e6300e45566319d2da28. The competition has not traded: data/season-2026/forward/leaderboard.json reports cycles_completed 0 with 10 strategies all still at $10,000.00, realized_pnl 0.0 and 0 fills, and data/season-2026/forward/{trades,intents,marks}.jsonl are each 0 lines. scripts/verify.py was executed here and reports passed 1198, errors 0 (its checks array lists 12 V1/V2 messages phrased as the failures they did not find, so the string 'missing provenance' in that output means the opposite), and `python3 -m pytest tests -q` reports 39 passed. A new data/season-2026/backtest/results.json records a candle-replay backtest that states its own assumptions in the artifact: signal on bar t close, fill at bar t+1 open with no look-ahead, candle-proxied touches that are explicitly NOT book-verified, a default 2-cent limit buffer capped at 0.99, cumulative-volume gates and signed slippage.

Research desk with two layers that are not allowed to blur: (1) the Nobel Prize record — prizes, laureates, the official motivation and the other names on the public nomination list where that list is open — and (2) a simulated Kalshi competition of 2,011 paper participants (11 primary plus a 2,000-strategy research program in 20 batches of 100) with predeclared strategies, run on the same stored Kalshi prices and the same starting cash. Every family has a research card (question, mechanism, information channels, predicted sign, falsification condition, verified sources) and is judged by predeclared rules against a random-entry null and, where one exists, a matched placebo, with the Program board as the working leaderboard and the Research view as the verdict table. The collector uses only the Python standard library and the public Kalshi Trade API (no key, no paid endpoint), and a full rebuild from the stored snapshot takes about 5 minutes. Simulated names, orders, positions and P&L are not Kalshi users and not real fills, and the project is not affiliated with Kalshi, Nobel Prize Outreach or the Nobel Foundation.

Independent archive of the official Nobel Prize records, not affiliated with Nobel Prize Outreach or the Nobel Foundation. It lists prizes by subject, the official motivation for each award, laureate details published by the Nobel Prize API (v2 preferred, v1 as a cross-check, disagreements flagged rather than silently rewritten) and — where the nomination archive is open — the other people and organizations named on the official nomination list, never guessed. Every row links back to nobelprize.org. Its committed verification report records 682 prize records (633 awarded, 49 not awarded), 1,018 laureate entities and 1,026 award slots, with 2026 prizes omitted because on 2026-09-24 the official physics list said the 2026 physics prize had not been awarded and would be announced 2026-10-06 at the earliest.

Fork of the GEMSDOE repository and site re-pointed at this repository, pursuing a different submission strategy: it treats the public leaderboard itself as the instrument, measuring five scored files (0.1563, 0.1560, 0.1193, 0.1152 and 0.0830, all committed and re-hashed) against a 0.3049 leader, and adds a topographic scarp channel the 19 official bands lack. Its instrument ranking finds the SGMC-gap proxy anti-ranks the board (Spearman minus 0.8) while the masked catalogue orders it at plus 0.9; the hidden-truth density fit is published as falsified (smallest fitting tolerance 0.028 DTI, and it predicts 0.81 for the platform's own example), so no candidate ships on its say-so. The primary upload is a catalogue-hedge candidate adding the 54,533 catalogue pixels the shipped field missed, with literature-grounded dilational-setting candidates second. The root index.html redirects to a docs/ site with strategy, executive summary, source catalogue and candidate downloads. The committed pytest log at this head reports 1 failed, 550 passed and 6 skipped: the failure is the stale committed strategy.html page (documented in the irregularities register).

Evidence-backed working site for the DOE GEMS Prize Challenge on DrivenData — predicting geothermal faults in the GeoDAWN region under a distance-weighted Tversky metric, with a 19-band feature stack, a $300,000 prize pool split $50,000 initial and $250,000 final, ending 2026-12-03 at 11:59 p.m. UTC. STATUS.md at commit cf20e1b records session 23 (2026-09-22), which made it possible to generate a submission from the site itself in the browser with no install and no GPU: scripts/build_submission_payload.py encodes the adopted artifact into docs/submission_field.bin (a gems-rle-v1 uleb128 run-length stream, 532,174 bytes for 259,549 runs) with a manifest of grid, transform, EPSG, resolution, value counts, blob hash, artifact hash and 17 named checks, where --check re-derives the manifest from the raster and refuses if any pin drifted and --verify decodes the blob and compares float32 bits; docs/geotiff_writer.js is a dependency-free TIFF/ZIP writer in plain JS with real deflate, and the CLI and CI routes produce the same bytes. Session 21 closed the standing data-placement blocker in-sandbox: scripts/assemble_data_bridge.py reassembled all three official rasters from committed parts in 2.8 s with every part and whole-file sha256 verified (training_features.tif 418,912,844 B, labels.tif 425,830 B, sample_submission.tif 1,599,597 B), and scripts/prepare_data.py passed on the placed bytes (3292x3730, 19 bands, EPSG:32611, 100 m, aligned bounds, band tags present); scripts/baseline_submission.py then reproduced the committed submission.tif byte for byte (sha256 9f2577cff20d...) in about 17 minutes on 2 vCPU with no torch, and scripts/validate_submission.py passed all 8 checks on the fresh file. The scientific stance remains deliberately unhedged and unclaimed: all four block-holdout folds are committed and the memorisation-versus-transfer gap is reported as a spread rather than a point — fold 0 +0.0114, fold 1 -0.0070, fold 2 +0.0139, fold 3 +0.0093, mean +0.006904, spread 0.020935, positive in 3 of 4 — and because no fold reaches the 12 resampling units a readable block-bootstrap interval needs, the scorer itself flags every per-fold interval interval_readable false, so the honest reading is 'no clear memorisation, no clear transfer gain' with the full-grid 0.099859 (reproduced digit-for-digit on a runner) retained as the selection statistic. The pseudo-label contrast has fired twice and STATUS.md is explicit that quoting either fire alone would overstate the precision: the first gave held-out blocks 0.0806 to 0.1841 (+0.1036, P=0.999) on the new-fault-like proxy against 0.2113 to 0.1239 (-0.0874, P=0.001) on catalogue labels, and the second — same seed, same config, same partition, different runner — measured proxy +0.0677 and catalogue -0.1079, a roughly 0.036 run-to-run swing on the proxy arm of the same order as the +0.0313 union contrast itself; the two scopes also disagree in sign on the union (+0.0313 held-out against -0.0375 trained-on), which STATUS.md calls instability rather than a result. Against the paired block-bootstrap bar its own FIELD rule uses (P >= 0.95) the union contrast reaches P = 0.916 with an interval spanning zero on 8 scoreable blocks, so it is suggestive and not established, both verdicts carry shippable_evidence false, and the circularity is derived rather than asserted because the pseudo-label path is the same proxy_catalogue.tif the proxy truth is cut from. A third population is the disjoint 122,652-pixel union (60,988 + 61,664, disjointness verified at score time). STATUS.md also defuses one coincidence on purpose: the baseline's union held-out DTI of 0.197183 resembles the public leaderboard's top score of 0.1972, and the two are unrelated quantities. IRREGULARITY: the repository's own test suite fails at this head — `python3 -m pytest tests -q` gives 467 passed, 32 skipped and 1 FAILED (tests/test_site_pages.py::test_the_build_reproduces_the_committed_pages), and 32 of the skips are environment-gated rather than the 1 STATUS.md reports (IRR-102). The next-session ordering stands at item 8 (the only unbiased signal: enroll and first upload, against a bar of 0.2854 as of 2026-09-21), then 3, then 6, then firing the pseudo-label workflow for folds 1-3 so the pool crosses 12 units before the runner artifacts expire around 2026-10-03.

Copy of the GEMSDOE tree (413 files) pursuing a new-fault-first strategy: both prize phases score withheld new faults, so it supervises with an independent fault compilation, gives the classifier lineament geometry instead of raw pixels, unions structurally different detectors and selects the combination rule on held-out geography. The adopted candidate at this head is the k = 2 of 5 union (suggested Note nff-union-5), measuring proxy DTI 0.1897 on the measurement fold against 0.1747 for the 4-member union it replaces (paired block bootstrap probability 0.957 of beating it over 9 blocks, which the README calls a COARSE interval); the deep ensemble member alone scores 0.0999. The landing page and executive summary open with an in-browser submission builder that writes the exact single-band float32 GeoTIFF the competition dialog asks for, re-reads its own bytes and hands over the download with a unique file name and suggested Note; the root index.html redirects to the docs/ site on legacy main / Pages. Its charter table records all 16 standing asks as done and measured with pull requests merged and a green make-submission run; uploading still needs a DrivenData login this kind of sandbox lacks, and no score is claimed anywhere — every number is the SGMC surrogate, not the scored set.

Autonomous, evidence-first research engine that runs on a timer with no manual input: .github/workflows/think.yml fires on a */30 * * * * cron, reads official public data, appends to a claims ledger, regenerates the site and documentation and commits its own output, so a cycle that reads nothing still publishes and says so. A cycle is seven stages, each a separate module that can fail without stopping the others — a failed stage becomes an entry in the irregularity register and the cycle still publishes. Figures below are the repository's own regenerated AUTO:COUNTS block at commit 86cb822, cycle 25, generated 2026-09-23T01:42:26Z: 22,070 accepted claims in the ledger (6,963 under the schema-v2 strict trace contract and 15,107 under the legacy contract reported but not upgraded; 14,570 captured from accepted source reads, 7,477 derived by recorded arithmetic and 23 negative proofs of absence), 0 claims rejected by the evidence gate, 2,294 derived claims rechecked this cycle with 0 that no longer recompute, 163 topics in the library (6 new), 416 published insights, 1,110 forecasts issued this cycle and 655 scored against outcomes, 28 ideas in the competition with 1 promoted, 27 open irregularities (1 new), 31 registered sources of which 28 are verified by a recorded live read and 3 are currently blocked, 63 reads ok against 4 failed this cycle out of 67 planned with 0 refused by the cap, 3,346 forecasts waiting for an observation, 0 that can never be scored, 2,155,278 bytes read, and 0 manual inputs required. data/irregularities.json at the same timestamp records 123 irregularities registered in total — 6 critical, 72 warn and 45 info — of which 27 are open, 96 resolved and 21 standing, where standing means a structural limit that deliberately does not auto-resolve. The 350-test suite was executed in this audit and passed (350 passed in 13.40s).

An autonomous, offline-first research engine: six numbered sources (arXiv, Crossref, GitHub, NIST NVD, PyPI, USGS), eleven named deterministic critic rules, nine published scoring criteria, a contradiction register that is never silently resolved, and a CLI that serves, publishes, re-verifies and reports. README.md at commit c492159 describes the pipeline as Compete (six personas build candidate answers made only of quotations plus fixed scaffolding), Criticise (eleven named rules attack each candidate, each attack recording the rule that fired) and Refuse when a source cannot be quoted. The generated run-a467fa87a6df site data (2026-09-23T00:55:17Z) reports 300 claims, 93 documents, 222 competing candidates carrying 960 recorded criticisms, 147 derived open questions, 12 contradictions awaiting a human decision, 102 discoveries, 4 topics, 5 experiments, 28 failures and 46 registered irregularities (23 info, 15 warning, 0 error) of which 38 are open and 8 resolved. Substance is measured rather than asserted: 42 of 300 claims are dated, 252 are quantified, the mean substance score is 0.5166 and 39.67 percent of the corpus is substantive rather than metadata. The run's own change scan attempted 6 sources and scanned 2: arXiv, Crossref, USGS and NVD all returned policy_refused because their robots.txt could not be fetched over the sandbox's TLS egress and RFC 9309 2.3.1.4 requires a crawler to assume complete disallow in that case, so no request was made to those hosts; GitHub and PyPI scanned and returned 13 new items between them. Its link checker reports 126 links checked, 106 ok and 20 failed, and records that the check ran on a GitHub Actions runner with unrestricted egress so that 'unreachable' is not mistaken for a broken link. Its own checks block additionally reports 159 claims without a link and 10 unsupported claims. The test suite was executed here: 189 passed.

2026-08-07

Zero-dependency static web app that pulls live MLB game data straight from the public MLB StatsAPI and renders it in a Gameday-style scoreboard, re-implemented from scratch in vanilla HTML/CSS/JS with no build step, no frameworks and no API keys.…

Quoted preview: the first 247 characters, cut at a sentence boundary. The full sourced description is 3,034 characters — expand to read every word published in the directory and check it by hand.

Zero-dependency static web app that pulls live MLB game data straight from the public MLB StatsAPI and renders it in a Gameday-style scoreboard, re-implemented from scratch in vanilla HTML/CSS/JS with no build step, no frameworks and no API keys. Two views: a scoreboard showing every game for any date with live scores, inning, count, probable pitchers and W/L/S decisions, and a game view showing who is at bat, who is pitching, the count, outs, runners on base, on-deck and in-the-hole hitters, pitch counts, last play, inning-by-inning linescore, full box score and the complete play-by-play timeline with pitch-by-pitch detail. A third surface is devoted to replay: an all-games Replay Feed page pulls review events from every game on the schedule — manager challenges, crew chief reviews, umpire reviews and ABS pitch challenges — with game link, inning, challenging team, reason, outcome, batter/pitcher context and a three-row review score tracker (Before review, Possible after, Actual after) that explicitly refuses to treat the possible score as a prediction, marks Before/Possible as not observed when the page opens only after resolution, and never assigns a later end-of-plate-appearance score to an earlier pitch review. A Runs at Risk mechanism decides from the data rather than the review type whether the call on the field credited runs to the very event under review — a run counts only when the official payload has a runners[] record with details.isScoringEvent true whose details.playIndex matches the reviewed event — and fires once per review with a chime, an optional desktop notification and a persistent banner, never predicting the ruling. Review detection reads the API's own status registry rather than matching the words challenge/review, because that missed crew-chief reviews whose official detailedState is 'Instant Replay'. The challenge counters are the official StatsAPI counters read as-is (review.away/home.used/remaining and gameData.absChallenges.away/home), never counts derived from feed events, never zero-filled when absent, and a used-counter that moves backwards between polls is flagged on the row as an irregularity instead of being silently corrected. A two-sided hit forecast compounds the batter's and pitcher's season rates by log5 with real platoon splits, recent form, head-to-head history, same-game familiarity and the live count into a single probability with a matchup tier and per-driver point adjustments. Auto-refresh runs at 500ms for the full feed with a 250ms status watcher for the whole slate, and a lean play-by-play probe at 250ms while a review is in flight. LIMITATION: the offline test suites run here — 14 of the 15 tools/*-test.mjs suites pass — but tools/smoke-test.mjs reports 8 checks failed in this sandbox because it fetches the live statsapi.mlb.com teams directory, gameStatus registry and review-status projection and this audit environment has no egress to that host, so that result is an environment limit rather than evidence of a repository defect.

2026-08-11

Searchable web viewer and reproducible archive pipeline for MLB regular-season and postseason games from 2014 forward, using exactly one source: HTTPS responses from MLB's own statsapi.mlb.com domain.…

Quoted preview: the first 201 characters, cut at a sentence boundary. The full sourced description is 2,491 characters — expand to read every word published in the directory and check it by hand.

Searchable web viewer and reproducible archive pipeline for MLB regular-season and postseason games from 2014 forward, using exactly one source: HTTPS responses from MLB's own statsapi.mlb.com domain. It does not invent, estimate, silently repair or manually enter a score, name, play or measurement — the raw download is the exact official response body and the human-readable files are deterministic projections of fields in that response. It browses the official schedule by date and includes MLB sportId=1 game types R, F, D, L and W (regular season and postseason) while excluding Spring Training, exhibitions and the All-Star Game, renders line scores, decisions, every play, pitch and action details, runner movements and batting and pitching box scores, searches a game's official descriptions and player names in the browser, and downloads a readable .txt report, the complete raw .json or an event-oriented .ndjson without a server or account. A bulk archive runs from January 1, 2014 through any requested end date with retries, rate limiting, resume support, provenance checks and checksums, live games refresh automatically using the wait interval returned in MLB's feed, and while a game is Live the pipeline logs transient official events — score changes, official-scorer ruling changes, scoring-pending rulings and boundary calls — into a per-game Event log tab stored locally in the browser so it can be reopened after the event or the game is over. A response is accepted only if the URL is HTTPS on the exact statsapi.mlb.com host, the response is successful JSON, it contains MLB Advanced Media's provenance notice, and a game feed's returned gamePk matches the requested gamePk. The site has no sample scores and no fallback dataset: if the official endpoint cannot be reached or does not return a value, the interface says so. The repository does not relicense MLB data — every saved record keeps MLB Advanced Media's returned notice, the exact source URL, the UTC retrieval time and a SHA-256 digest, and users remain responsible for complying with MLB's terms. The site is dependency-free static HTML, CSS and JavaScript in docs/ that requests data directly from MLB in the visitor's browser, with no third-party analytics, proxy, database, generated game content, logo or team artwork. Both test suites were executed here: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` reports Ran 6 tests, OK, and `node --test tests/test_eventlog.mjs` reports 15 tests, 15 pass, 0 fail.

Honest about limits: error-type adjustment and pending calibration switch on only once enough captured plays settle.

Autonomous NBA betting-strategy research and paper-trading competition lab that cross-verifies real public data, turns public claims into testable hypotheses, backtests them against verified historical prices where those exist and forward-tests them where they do not. It runs 27 system-generated strategies (NBA-001 through NBA-027) with a $1,000 virtual bankroll each over a competition window of 2026-09-20 to 2027-09-19, staking at 25% Kelly capped at 3% per bet, collecting ESPN schedule, scores and current odds, the ESPN injury board, Kalshi public market data and NBA.com/stats team and player logs, and modeling chronological Elo (MOV-adjusted, with season carryover), rolling pace and efficiency totals and rest, back-to-back, road-trip and time-zone features. Signal-quality results are published as signal quality and never mixed into backtest or forward PnL records. IRR-04Warning—GEMSDOE repository size grew from 6,695 KB to 394,791 KB (~386 MB) in one day
Full recorded detail

Measured from GitHub's own size field on consecutive audits. Per the repository's README (session 9, 2026-09-17): the three official competition rasters are now committed as sha256-pinned git parts under data/bridge/ and reassembled into data/. This is a deliberate design choice, but '384 MB of rasters in git' is the kind of growth that runs into GitHub's repository size guidance (soft warning at ~5 GB, 100 MB per-file warning) and makes clones slow. Suggest Git LFS or a release asset instead.

IRR-07Warning—GitHub's three timestamp fields do not always agree with each other
Full recorded detail

Observed at the 2026-09-17 snapshot, measured from GitHub's own fields: GEMSDOE pushed_at (2026-09-17T21:42:44Z) is ~2.4 h later than its newest main commit (8f7fc51, 19:21:20Z) because of arena/* session branch activity, while its updated_at (19:21:26Z) sits ~2.4 h BEFORE pushed_at. ScheduleFreeTime pushed_at (21:42:58Z) is 215 s later than its newest main commit (cdef1db, 21:39:23Z) and its updated_at (21:39:28Z) is 210 s before pushed_at. Tradingview-pinescript-editor updated_at (2026-08-19T04:53:13Z) is ~9.1 days later than its pushed_at (2026-08-10T01:43:17Z); Itinerary-Korea is ~7.8 h later and HotelSeoulRoughdraft1 ~6.2 h. Note that NBAInjuryReport showed pushed_at 97 s EARLIER than its newest main commit earlier in the same audit and had resolved to +6 s by the final read — these fields are eventually consistent, not transactionally consistent. This directory publishes created / firstCommit / lastCommit / pushedAt / updatedAt separately rather than picking one, because each answers a different question.

open—
IRR-20Warning—Descriptions are README-sourced and can go stale between refreshes
Full recorded detail

Every description is extracted from the repository's own README.md or generated site, which means a push that changes a README without changing the facts this directory records leaves the wording behind. Concrete example found on 2026-09-17: BathTubOverflowSF's 'Current research snapshot' header still reads 'Checked through September 16, 2026 / sixteen waves' while the same README documents waves 17, 18 and 19. The generator re-reads every README whose commit count moved, but prose still needs a human check. The 2026-09-18 audit found the same failure again, in four places, and it is the single largest risk to this directory: StockPaperSim was still described as a README-only placeholder after it had become a full site, KalshiPaperSim's quoted AUTO:COUNTS totals had all moved, NBAInjuryReport's test counts (188/52) no longer matched its README (212/71), and TradingViewTheLeap's README pointer named a capture file that a later capture had superseded. A numeric token still being present somewhere in a long README is not proof that a claim is current, so tools/audit_descriptions.py only narrows the search - a human or model pass has to read each one.

The 2026-09-17 snapshot names 27 of 30 team blocks (CLE, DET, LAL absent).

IRR-30Warning—Stale narrative, not missing repositories, is the dominant error mode
Full recorded detail

This audit re-read every API-derived field for all 39 entries and found drift in ten, but only one was structurally wrong (WoWForever, a site the previous audit could not see). The rest were descriptions that had quietly stopped being true: one false classification (StockPaperSim), one set of entirely superseded counters (KalshiPaperSim), one set of stale test counts (NBAInjuryReport), one omitted incident (NFLInjuryReport) and one lagging evidence pointer (TradingViewTheLeap). The generator keeps the numbers honest; nothing currently forces the prose to be re-read, so every future refresh should assume the prose is the stale part and check it against the repository rather than against the previous audit.

IRR-50Warning—The overlay stamped only a timestamp, so a repo that moved after its prose was read was undetectable — fixed with a recorded commit SHA, which then caught five moves in one pass
Full recorded detail

Design defect found and fixed during this audit. README task #3 asked for a per-entry `lastVerified` date so a reader could tell which descriptions were re-read this cycle. That was implemented, and IRR-47 immediately proved it inadequate: ShoulderPain's default branch moved at 2026-09-20T02:42:19Z, 60 seconds after its stamp of 02:41:19Z was written, so the stamp said 're-read in the latest pass' about a repository state that no longer existed. A date answers 'when did someone look', not 'is what they wrote still what is there'. Every entry therefore now also carries `verifiedAtSha` — the 7-character default-branch commit its prose was read against — and tools/build_data.py compares it with the live `lastCommitSha` at build time, emitting `proseStale` per entry and `counts.proseStale` in aggregate. The comparison is exact and free: no re-reading required, and a future session can list every entry whose prose is provably behind its repository before deciding what to read. Entries whose prose was carried forward record the prior audit's SHA rather than being left blank, so a carried entry is stale only if its repository actually moved. MasterSite's own entry is the one deliberate exception: its description describes this directory rather than a fetched README, so it is stamped with the SHA current when the description was last written and will always read stale immediately after a push. Limitation that remains: a matching SHA proves the prose was read against those bytes, not that it is still *true* — a repository can be accurate at one commit and wrong at the next without either changing (IRR-04 to IRR-06). What each build actually reported, in order: the first build after `verifiedAtSha` was added flagged 3 entries behind their repositories — GEMSDOE (session 19 had landed, superseding the session-18 status the description quoted), SFWeather (session 14) and ShoulderPain (a fourth commit) — all three of which had been re-read and stamped earlier in the *same* session, so a timestamp stamp would have called them current. After those were re-read, the next build flagged exactly 1: DrugAnalysis, whose prose had been *carried* from the prior audit at 6cb77f6 and whose v21.1 pre-1980 spine landed mid-build at b6cdb8a. Re-reading it, the next build flagged 2 more — SFWeather again (36fa2e4 to 42f9892, an automated data-only refresh that left README.md untouched, so its prose was re-verified by line-by-line diff and re-stamped rather than rewritten) and VacationSchedule (83663f7 to 6488222, which changed every window it recommends; IRR-52). The build after that flagged a fifth, OLBG-Competition (a371b63 to fee61ea), which had added an entire ice hockey pipeline and moved its test count 126 to 144 (IRR-54). Across the second pass the gate fired seven times on six distinct repositories, every one caught mechanically. Note which entries the detector caught: GEMSDOE, SFWeather and ShoulderPain had been re-read minutes earlier in the same session, but DrugAnalysis and OLBG-Competition were *carried* forward from earlier passes — precisely the entries a human auditor would least think to re-check, and the ones whose prose had actually gone false. The rebuild that reports `0 provably behind their repo` is the pass's completion gate: it means all 44 descriptions were read at the commit the repository is still at, which no timestamp can assert. Reproduce: run `python3 tools/build_data.py` and read the `PROSE-STALE` lines; the check is `entry.verifiedAtSha == repo head SHA`, using the same `gitHead` value the live dataset already records for every entry.

IRR-53Info—GitHub Pages `status` is a live state machine, so a snapshot can record a healthy site as 'building' — the generator now waits and re-reads
Full recorded detail

Observed directly during this audit. The build that ran at 2026-09-20T02:57Z recorded `pagesBuilt: 43` of 44, the one exception being SFWeather with `pagesStatus: 'building'`. A re-read of GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/{repo}/pages for all 44 entries 12 seconds later returned `built` for every one, including SFWeather. The cause is not a defect in the repository or the directory: SFWeather's own automated nightly commit 42f9892 'data: refresh NOAA/NWS/NCEI/CPC sources [skip ci]' had landed at 02:57:39Z, seconds before the read, and GitHub Pages was mid-deploy. `status` is therefore a transient property of the deployment at the instant of the read, not a durable property of the repository — the same class of asynchrony as `size` (IRR-04 to IRR-06), but with a worse failure mode, because publishing '43 of 44 built' reads as 'one site is broken' when the truth is 'one site was rebuilding'. Fix applied to tools/build_data.py: when a Pages read returns any status other than 'built', the generator now re-reads that endpoint up to four times at six-second intervals before recording it, prints how many re-reads it needed, and still records a genuinely non-built status as-is with a warning if it persists — so a real broken build is never masked, only a transient one. Any future audit whose dataset reports fewer built sites than listed entries should re-read the endpoint before treating it as a finding.

IRR-55Critical—GitHub credentials expired mid-audit at ~2026-09-20T03:07Z, blocking the live re-verification, the push and the pull request for about two hours (resolved 04:54Z)
Full recorded detail

Recorded because it bounds what this audit can claim, and because the failure is silent in a dangerous way. The dataset published here was generated successfully at 2026-09-20T03:06:19Z (44 entries, 2,360 commits, all 44 Pages builds reporting `built`, 0 entries behind their repository head). Roughly one minute later every GitHub read began failing: `gh auth status` reports 'The github.com token in GH_TOKEN is no longer valid', `gh api repos/buffedlizard55-lab/MasterSite` returns HTTP 401 'Bad credentials', and — the part that makes this easy to miss — a plain unauthenticated `curl https://api.github.com/users/buffedlizard55-lab` also returns 401 rather than the usual 200, so the sandbox's egress credential itself is what expired, not a per-request token. Three consequences, stated plainly rather than papered over. (1) tools/verify_live.py was run after the expiry and returned 51 checks with every one a 'MISMATCH ... committed=200 live=401'; that output is an artifact of the auth failure and NOT a finding about the data, so tools/last_live_verify.json was restored from git to the last valid run (2026-09-20T01:17:26Z, 582 checks, 0 hard mismatches). A future session must not read the 401 run as 44 broken entries. (2) tools/audit_descriptions.py could not be re-run at all because it fetches each repository's README to diff against the published description; tools/last_description_audit.json is therefore a labelled PARTIAL artifact whose `unresolved` list predates the final edits to ShoulderPain and Elections. (3) `git push` failed with 'could not read Username for https://github.com', so at that moment the branch existed only as a local commit on arena/01a0bc90-mastersite, with no pull request opened and nothing merged. Two mitigations were verified while access still worked, so the published content itself is sound: the generator's own prose-staleness gate reported `0 provably behind their repo` for all 44 entries at the 03:06:19Z build, and no published artifact reads either verifier output file (tools/build_data.py, tools/build_verification.py, index.html and app.js were each grepped). Reproduce the failure: `gh auth status`, then `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://api.github.com/users/buffedlizard55-lab`. Resolution needs the account owner to reconnect GitHub; on reconnect the required sequence is re-run verify_live.py, re-run audit_descriptions.py, then push and open the pull request. Because these repositories commit continuously (IRR-42, IRR-50, IRR-52, IRR-54), the reconnect must also re-run build_data.py first — the 03:06:19Z snapshot will very likely already be behind by then. RESOLVED 2026-09-20: the owner reconnected GitHub at approximately 04:54Z. The prescribed sequence was then followed exactly rather than pushing the stale snapshot — a full build_data.py rebuild first, which reported 4 entries behind and grew total commits from 2,360 to 2,398, then re-read and rewrite, then rebuild again, repeating until the gate reported 0 provably behind (four builds, ten further moves, IRR-61), then build_verification.py, then all three verifiers, all of which ran clean: verify_live.py 582 of 582 checks with 0 hard mismatches and 0 drift, audit_descriptions.py 0 entries needing manual confirmation against 7 documented exceptions, and audit_kind.py 44 of 44 re-derived with 0 disagreements. The bogus 401 run and the PARTIAL description-audit artifact were both replaced by real runs. Final snapshot 2026-09-20T05:15:50Z, 2,413 commits, 62 irregularities.

All four were re-read against their repositories' own files and rewritten between 04:57Z and 05:04Z. ShoulderPain is now on its fifth verified state inside one audit and StockPaperSim's move is the sharpest case: IRR-60 had been written minutes earlier asserting that StockPaperSim's stamp 12fd8f2 equalled its current head SHA, which was true when checked and false by the next build, so IRR-60 itself had to be corrected — an irregularity entry invalidated by the churn it was describing. Operational consequence recorded for future sessions: re-reading and rebuilding must be treated as one loop that repeats until the gate reports 0, not as two steps, and any claim of the form 'repository X is unchanged' has a shelf life of minutes. IRR-62Warning—This directory's own GEMSDOE description quoted one replicate of a twice-fired measurement, which its source explicitly forbids — caught only because an evidence-only commit was read instead of waved through
Full recorded detail

A defect in this directory's prose, not in the repository's, and the most instructive catch of the audit because the mechanism that found it was not the one designed to. The prose-staleness gate reported GEMSDOE behind at 3093c6b; the intervening commit 0ca1466 is 'evidence(pseudo-labels): fold 0 with SGMC pseudo, paired vs no-pseudo baseline [skip ci]', touching 6 files at +1/-1 each and changing neither README.md nor STATUS.md. By the shortcut reasoning recorded for SFWeather in IRR-61 — automated commit, prose files untouched, therefore re-stamp — this would have been waved through. Instead the six files were fetched, because they are evidence rather than data refreshes, and data/evidence/pseudo_labels/fold0_two_population_contrast.json now reports verdict GAIN_ON_THE_COMBINED_SURROGATE with proxy +0.067719 and catalogue -0.107890. Those are not the +0.1036 (P=0.999) / -0.0874 (P=0.001) / TRADE_OFF_PROXY_GAINS_CATALOGUE_LOSSES values the published description quoted. Reading STATUS.md — byte-identical at both commits, so the gate could not have flagged it — explains why: the measurement has fired twice, and STATUS.md states that 'The effect is the size of the replicate noise. The first fire measured proxy +0.1036 (P = 0.999) and catalogue -0.0874 (P = 0.001); this fire, same seed, same config, same partition, different runner, measured proxy +0.0677 and catalogue -0.1079', a ~0.036 run-to-run swing on the proxy arm of the same order as the +0.0313 union contrast itself. It adds that the two scopes disagree in sign on the union (+0.0313 held-out vs -0.0375 trained-on), which is 'instability, not a result', that read against the bar the repository's own FIELD rule uses (paired block bootstrap P >= 0.95) the union contrast reaches only P = 0.916 with an interval spanning zero on 8 scoreable blocks, making it 'suggestive, not established', and — the operative sentence — 'Eight blocks is not a sample. Both fires' numbers are in git history; the second overwrote the first's files, so quoting either one alone would overstate the precision.' The description did precisely that: it quoted the first fire alone and presented one derived verdict as the finding, so it was accurate about a number and misleading about the measurement. Corrected to carry both fires, the replicate swing, the scope sign disagreement, the P = 0.916 reading and the 0.197183-vs-0.1972 coincidence STATUS.md defuses on purpose. Two lessons recorded for future sessions. First, `verifiedAtSha` matching proves the prose was read against those bytes, not that it is true — the failing text had been read at 3093c6b minutes earlier and STATUS.md had not changed at all, so no SHA comparison could ever have flagged it; only reading the evidence the prose quotes did. Second, an evidence-only `[skip ci]` commit is not automatically benign: unlike a data refresh, evidence files are what a research description quotes, so they must be opened. Reproduce: GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/GEMSDOE/contents/data/evidence/pseudo_labels/fold0_two_population_contrast.json?ref=main and compare its `verdict` and `verdict_reason` with the pseudo-label table in STATUS.md item 2.

Full recorded detail

GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/{repo}/pages returns status built, build_type legacy and source {branch main, path /} for all ten repositories added this pass. In legacy mode GitHub builds the branch root with Jekyll, so a repository with no root index.html publishes a rendered README rather than its built site. Three are in that state at the heads this pass read: LSTARENGY (built page at docs/index.html, 27,155 bytes), RGENGY (built site in site/, and .github/workflows/pages.yml uploads it with actions/upload-pages-artifact path: site then actions/deploy-pages — an Actions deployment path that a legacy build_type ignores) and StokEngineer (docs/index.html, 71,004 bytes, with README.md promising the site 'after Pages deploy'). All three are classified stub by the mechanical rule in tools/audit_kind.py — an index.html at the published path — even though each holds a real built site one directory down. Two repositories hit the same configuration and solved it with a forwarder, so they classify as app: SABERENGY's root index.html is a 146-byte meta-refresh to docs/ (commit f874c69 also corrected the README's Pages instructions to match the live config) and NFLPARLAYCOMP's is a 2,429-byte page that redirects to docs/ and explains the legacy build in its own body, added by commit b7e41a4 'Make the Pages site reachable; flag the Pages source misconfiguration'. NFLPARLAYCOMP's docs/OPERATIONS.md and SABERENGY's commit message both record the same root cause: PUT /repos/{owner}/{repo}/pages returns 403 Resource not accessible by integration for the repository's own token, so the intended GitHub Actions source cannot be set. The remaining four (Coupons, FLABSENGY, FUTURESCOMMODITIES, ParlaySports) have a real root index.html and publish correctly. Reproduce: GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/RGENGY/pages and GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/RGENGY/contents/index.html?ref=main (HTTP 404).

IRR-81Warning—StokEngineer's own pass log recorded a fix that had not been made, and its README claimed more verified sources than its registry held
Full recorded detail

Two self-documented accuracy failures in one repository, both corrected before this pass read them. (1) PASSES.md's Session 2 table states that simulation_engine.py's demo returned roughly 21,000 percent Sim ROI and that 'PASSES.md claimed this was fixed; it was not'. The recorded root cause is that field lineups were generated from a player pool the same size as the roster, so every 'field' lineup was a permutation of all players and tied the user lineup; the recorded fix rewrites field generation so the pool exceeds the roster, adds an illustrative GPP payout curve with documented rake, adds a guard that raises when pool size equals lineup size, and replaces the ad-hoc covariance with a PSD projection. A pass log asserting a fix that the code did not contain is the same failure class this directory exists to catch (IRR-25, IRR-33, IRR-65), found here by the repository itself. (2) README.md at commit ee74de1 said 'See src/data/sources_verified.json for 40+ verified sources' while that file held 38 source entries; commit 1842144 ('fix: re-verify all sources live; fix ROI-understated bug, pricing labels, dead links') removed the numeric claim, and the registry at head 9404a80 holds 38 entries across six categories plus 7 irregularity records, up from 4. This directory publishes the counted 38 and quotes the defect rather than summarising it away. Reproduce: GET /repos/buffedlizard55-lab/StokEngineer/compare/ee74de1...9404a80.

Elections does have a root index.html, so it is classified app; the errored status means the latest deployment did not succeed, and this directory records that as-is rather than inferring either that the URL is dead (GitHub continues serving the last successful build) or that it is healthy. The defect was real and was not the transient 'building' state IRR-53 describes — it persisted through four automatic re-reads over twenty minutes and two consecutive failed builds — but it is closed, and the 2026-09-22 snapshot's pagesBuilt 60 of 61 is superseded by 62 of 62 at the 2026-09-23 refresh and by 63 of 63 in the snapshot that ships, once NFL-PLAYER-PROP-SIM was listed and SelfLearn's transient 'building' state cleared. Recorded rather than deleted: this is the first Pages build failure this directory has caught and the first it has watched recover, which is the evidence that the four-read retry loop distinguishes a genuinely broken build from a deploy in progress in both directions.

No API-derived field is touched by either change. (2) A CURATED REPOSITORY STILL CANNOT BE LISTED. PRICINGEXPERT was created at 2026-09-22T23:01:18Z, was seen and correctly withheld by the 23:32:49Z refresh ('no curated entry in tools/overlay.json — skipped from the directory'), and now has a complete overlay entry written from its own contents, but render_overlay_only iterates the sites already in the snapshot and can never add one. The consequence is visible in the published data: counts.unlisted still gives its reason as 'no curated entry in tools/overlay.json yet', which stopped being true the moment the entry was written. Both are corrected by the next full build, and neither was hand-edited, because counts.unlisted is generated. (3) THE GATE CAN REPORT AN ENTRY THAT IS AHEAD OF THE SNAPSHOT AS STALE. MasterSelfLearn commits its own output on a */30 cron, so it moved from fe488ed (the head the 23:32:49Z snapshot recorded) to eda549e while this pass was reading it, and the description was written against eda549e. proseStale is computed as verifiedAtSha != headSha, so the entry reports stale even though its prose was read at a NEWER commit than the snapshot knows about. That is the conservative output and it is left as-is: re-stamping the entry back to fe488ed to silence the gate would mean claiming the prose was read at a commit it was not read at. Only a full refresh resolves it.

open—
IRR-87Warning—counts.proseReReadLatestPass measures 'shares the newest stamp', not 're-read this pass', so a pass that stamps as it goes publishes an undercount and the UI then calls brand-new entries 'carried'
Full recorded detail

Found while writing this pass's summary, and fixed in the data rather than in the counter. The field is computed as the number of entries whose lastVerified equals max(lastVerified) over all entries. That is only equal to 're-read in the latest pass' if a pass stamps everything it touches with one timestamp. This pass did not: it authored or re-read twelve entries at three instants as repositories moved under it (ten at 2026-09-22T23:20:00Z, NFLPARLAYCOMP at 23:45:00Z after IRR-82, SelfLearn and MasterSelfLearn at 23:50:00Z after IRR-84), so the counter reported 2 and the generator's own summary line printed 'prose: 2 re-read this pass, 59 carried'. The consequence is not only a wrong total. app.js derives the per-entry badge from the same comparison, so ten entries written for the first time in this pass — including nine repositories that did not exist 24 hours earlier — rendered as 'Prose carried', a state VERIFICATION.md section 4a defines as 'the prose is from an earlier pass'. The distortion was already known: app.js's legend carries the disclaimer 'This is not a count of all descriptions re-read during a multi-hour audit', and the previous pass recorded it in README.md rather than resolving it ('proseReReadLatestPass: 1 counts only entries sharing the single newest lastVerified timestamp, so it understates a multi-hour pass'). RESOLUTION: the counter's definition was left alone, because making it mean what its name says would require a pass boundary that neither overlay.json nor data/sites.js carries, and inventing one would be a new way to be wrong. Instead the twelve stamps were normalized to the pass stamp 2026-09-22T23:50:00Z — the newest of the three, so no entry's stamp moved backwards — and the published figures are now the honest ones: 11 of 61 listed entries re-read this pass (the twelfth, PRICINGEXPERT, is curated but cannot be listed without a full build, IRR-86), 50 carried, 21 provably behind. The per-entry read instants were not lost: each entry's verifiedBasis still names the commit it was read against and what was opened, which is where that detail belongs. Normalization does not weaken staleness detection, because proseStale compares verifiedAtSha with the recorded head SHA and never consults lastVerified. DURABLE RULE for the next session: stamp every entry a pass touches with one pass-completion timestamp, and put per-entry read times in verifiedBasis; a pass that stamps as it goes will under-report its own work and mislabel its newest entries as carried. Reproduce: python3 -c "import json;print(sorted({e['lastVerified'] for e in json.load(open('tools/overlay.json'))['entries'].values()}))" — before the fix this printed three 2026-09-22 stamps and the counter read 2; after it, one stamp and the counter reads 11. REFINED BY THE CASE IT DID NOT ANTICIPATE. The rule recorded above — stamp every entry a pass touches with one pass-completion timestamp — assumes a pass runs in one continuous window. This one did not: credentials expired at 23:55Z (IRR-85) and were restored after 00:44Z, so the pass has two stamp cohorts, 2026-09-22T23:50:00Z for the twelve entries read before the expiry and 2026-09-23T00:55:00Z for the six read after it. Normalizing all eighteen to the later stamp was considered and rejected, because six of the first cohort had gone PROSE-STALE in the interval — their repositories moved while the audit was blocked — so claiming they were verified at 00:55Z would assert a currency that the gate itself contradicts. The durable rule is therefore narrower than first written: stamp one timestamp per continuous working window, never per entry, and never bump a stamp forward for an entry the gate reports as behind. counts.proseReReadLatestPass consequently reports the newest cohort only (6), and the honest figure for the whole pass — 18 entries read or re-read, 12 before the expiry and 6 after — is stated here and in README.md rather than inferred from the counter.

IRR-108Critical—Two entries in this directory's own dataset carried verifiedAtSha values that are not commit SHAs, making the staleness gate permanently unsatisfiable
Full recorded detail

build_data.py sets proseStale by comparing entries[*].verifiedAtSha with the live default-branch head SHA. Two curated entries held non-SHA strings: KalshiPaperSim held 'AUTO:COUNTS' and MasterSite held 'main'. Neither can ever equal a seven-character commit SHA, so both were reported PROSE-STALE by every build regardless of whether their prose had actually been read at head, and the UI badged them with 'read at AUTO:COUNTS' and 'read at main'. Two consequences: the gate silently became a permanent work item rather than a measurement, and the honest question the field exists to answer - was this prose read against these bytes? - had no answer for the two most-churned entries in the directory. The auto-generated-block label and the branch name had leaked into a field defined as a commit. Both are now resolved from executed evidence: KalshiPaperSim was re-read at 8a359bd with its suite run (157 tests, 157 pass) and MasterSite is stamped b1c9765 with the reason recorded in its verifiedBasis. A follow-up guard belongs in build_data.py: warn when an entry's verifiedAtSha is set but does not match ^[0-9a-f]{7,40}$, so a leaked label can never again masquerade as a stamp. Reproduce: inspect tools/overlay.json entries KalshiPaperSim and MasterSite in any commit before this pass, then compare with the printout 'PROSE-STALE KalshiPaperSim description read at AUTO:COUNTS' and 'PROSE-STALE MasterSite description read at main'.

IRR-131Critical—38 public Pages repositories were absent from the published directory between the 2026-10-01 snapshot and this refresh
Full recorded detail

The 2026-10-01 snapshot recorded 108 public repositories, all Pages-enabled, and published 107 of them. A fresh API census on 2026-10-11 found 146 public repositories, all 146 reporting a Pages site, so 38 public Pages repositories were reaching the public web with no entry, no description and no source row here. The cause is mechanical rather than mysterious: the account gained 33 GEMSDOE-family repositories and 3 scoring-discrepancy repositories between 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-08 (created_at), and tools/build_data.py deliberately skips any repository that has no curated entry in tools/overlay.json, listing it under counts.unlisted rather than describing it. No hallucinated value was published in the gap - the repositories were simply missing. All 38 now carry curated prose read at the recorded head SHA, with the README, Pages and contents endpoints recorded in each verifiedBasis. Re-confirm by hand with https://api.github.com/users/buffedlizard55-lab/repos?per_page=100&page=2 (the account now needs two pages) and by comparing counts.publicRepos with counts.sites.

IRR-133Info—MALTASUPPLEMENTAL reported `building` in a mid-session census read and `built` in the final snapshot
Full recorded detail

A mid-session census read at 2026-10-11T02:5xZ returned Pages status `building` for this repository, about three minutes after its newest push (pushed_at 2026-10-11T02:51:09Z); the final full build minutes later recorded `built` (source `main /`), which is the value published in the 2026-10-11T03:01:01Z snapshot. This is the transient behaviour tools/build_data.py already guards against by re-reading a non-`built` Pages status up to four times before recording it, and it is why a single mid-pass read is not treated as a regression. It also shows why a snapshot timestamp must travel with any status claim: `building` was true at 02:5xZ and false at 03:01Z. Source: https://api.github.com/repos/buffedlizard55-lab/MALTASUPPLEMENTAL/pages .

Full recorded detail

Thirty-three GEMSDOE-family repositories were created between 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-08 (created_at): GEMSDOE28-GEMSDOE54 and 55GEMSDOE-62GEMSDOE. Each is an independent lane of the same DrivenData #306 task with its own README-level charter, and several state mutually exclusive conclusions about which artifact should be submitted - for example 58GEMSDOE blocks its own submission while its neighbour 57GEMSDOE records download and submit as OK, and each repository's verdict is scoped to its own lane. The directory lists all of them with the repository's own verdict and takes no position on which is correct. This volume is why a family grouping view is worth adding. IRR-137Info—tools/build_data.py could not derive a site kind at all until 2026-10-11 (latent fail-closed bug, now fixed)
Full recorded detail

has_entry_point() probes GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{published path}/index.html with Accept: application/vnd.github.raw to decide app-vs-stub when the overlay does not state a kind. api_get() unconditionally passed the response body through json.loads, so a successful raw read of an HTML file raised JSONDecodeError, fell into the generic `except Exception` branch, and returned HTTP status 0 instead of 200. has_entry_point() correctly refuses to guess on an unexpected status, so the whole build aborted with 'cannot derive site kind'. The path was never exercised before because all 107 existing overlay entries happen to carry an explicit `kind`; it fired on the first entry without one (55GEMSDOE, whose 241-byte root index.html redirects to docs/index.html). The 38 new entries in this pass therefore pin `kind` explicitly from their own official probe, and api_get() now returns the response text instead of raising when the body is not JSON, restoring the derivation path for future sessions. Reproduce the original failure by reverting the api_get() guard and running `python3 tools/build_data.py` with any entry lacking a kind. No published value was ever derived from the broken path (it could only fail, never return a wrong status). Sources: tools/build_data.py (api_get, has_entry_point); https://api.github.com/repos/buffedlizard55-lab/55GEMSDOE/contents/index.html?ref=main returns HTTP 200 with a 241-byte HTML redirect; tools/audit_kind.py independently re-derives all 145 kinds from the same endpoints.

IRR-140Warningtools/build_data.pyThe generator read GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/pages twice per repository (once for the account census, once for the entry). That endpoint is the one GitHub throttles hardest: after several full refreshes in one session the endpoint began answering HTTP 403 with a secondary-rate-limit body while /rate_limit still reported 5000/5000 core calls remaining. The reads are now cached per repository (one call instead of two), 403 rate-limit responses are retried with backoff instead of being recorded as 'not a Pages site', and the run fails closed rather than publishing a short list.mitigatedrecorded source

  • Account-level reads: GET /users/buffedlizard55-lab/repos · GET /users/kanlerxz87-cyber/repos.
  • Descriptions are curated in tools/overlay.json from each repository's own files and carry a stamp (verifiedAtSha) plus the endpoint that was read; a stamp behind the snapshot head is flagged on the row and queued in FLAGGED.md.
  • Generated by tools/build_master_list.py; scripts/validate §M fails when this page drifts from the snapshot or a row loses its official link.
